Universe Creation

The founding brief this entire plan is built from — the non-negotiable creative DNA every season, episode, and AI-generation prompt must inherit.

Premise

One sentence, memorable enough to survive fifty seasons of drift.

A tailor's daughter from Aba rises inside the Big Tech company that once failed her mother — and has to decide, season after season, whether to burn it down or rebuild it into something that finally keeps its promises.

Required registers

The brief specifies these must all be present, baked into the plot rather than bolted onto it: Love, Romance, Infidelity, Suffering, Tragedy, Comedy, Wealth, Power, Intrigue, Secrets, Mystery, Suspense, Flashbacks.

Protagonist mandate

A woman, by design.

Adaeze "Ada" Obi carries the show. Every era's central mystery is discovered through her curiosity, and every romance, rivalry, and betrayal in the ensemble is ultimately measured against whether it helps or hurts the two hundred small businesses she started fighting for in Episode 1.

Scripting, Romance & Scene Mandate

Pacing, stakes cadence, romance integration, memory structure, and universal dialogue standards.

  • Cinematic 16:9 widescreen format — All episodes, shots, and AI prompts are designed and generated in 16:9 cinematic horizontal widescreen (1.78:1 / anamorphic framing). This format maximizes environmental storytelling (the sprawl of Aba markets, the gleaming high-rise glass of Victoria Island, and the bustle of Accra), enables expressive multi-character staging, and provides room for intimate, tension-filled two-shots during romantic and boardroom confrontations.
  • 3 to 7 scenes per episode — Every ~3-minute episode must be structured into between 3 and 7 distinct scenes with explicit sluglines (INT./EXT. location and time of day), ensuring dynamic visual pacing and rhythmic narrative transitions.
  • 1-in-4 life-or-death stakes cadence — Physical life-or-death peril (fires, structural collapses, armed robberies, sabotaged brakes) is strictly reserved for milestone punctuation episodes (one in every four episodes: E01, E04, E08, E12, E16, E20). The other 18 episodes center on high-stakes commercial, legal, and professional survival (vendor bankruptcy, audit traps, leaked ledgers, career suspension, boardroom voting pacts), grounding the show in authentic market and tech realities.
  • Romance woven into every episode — Every single episode must feature an organic romantic beat: an unspoken yearning, a stolen glance, a late-night call, jealous friction, or a moment of mutual vulnerability. The slow-burn connection between Ada & Tobi, the forbidden romance between Bubbles & Chidi, and the unraveling engagement of Tobi & Yewande must be baked directly into the daily plot.
  • At least one flashback event in 4 episodes — Backstory is dramatized directly rather than dumped in exposition; pivotal history (such as the 2019 Aba fire, the original escrow code drafting, or the secret board pact) must appear as dramatized flashback events at least once every four episodes.
  • Dialogue on every shot (100% coverage) — Every single shot must carry spoken dialogue or an internal thought in the heart of the character (indicated via parentheticals such as (internal thought) or (in her heart)). No shot is left silent.

Education mandate

Three teachable moments per episode, dramatized rather than narrated.

  • One business tip — a real, usable piece of small-business practice, demonstrated through action (a character actually reconciling stock, actually opening a storefront, actually escrowing a payment) rather than stated as advice to camera.
  • One relationship insight — about trust, loyalty, honesty, or boundaries — earned by what a character does in the episode, not delivered as a moral at the end.
  • One Good Strategy / Bad Strategy lesson — drawn from Richard Rumelt's book and dramatized through a decision a character actually makes, not quoted as a business-book aphorism. See below.

The Good Strategy / Bad Strategy mandate

Baked in as often as possible — every season card and every Season 1 episode carries one lesson from Richard Rumelt's book, and Era 3 dramatizes the whole thing.

Rumelt's core idea: good strategy has a kernel — a diagnosis of the real obstacle, a guiding policy for dealing with it, and coherent action that actually carries the policy out. Bad strategy is what's left when a leader skips the diagnosis and just lists goals, or hides the missing plan behind impressive-sounding language ("fluff"). Every Ofia character eventually runs into this distinction — a merchant confusing "sell more" for a plan, a rival confusing ambition for strategy, a founder confusing growth for health — and the show never states the lesson outright; a character's choice demonstrates it instead.

The Jobs/Apple parallel — Ofia's turnaround decade

Era 3 (Seasons 21–30) is built as a direct homage to Steve Jobs's 1997 return to Apple, restaged inside Ofia.

  • The sprawl (S21–22). Chasing a Series C, Ofia expands into dozens of half-finished products across every market that asks for one — the platform equivalent of Apple's bloated pre-1997 lineup. No diagnosis, just goals.
  • The ouster (S23). The Adamu Ledger scandal gives the board its excuse: Chiamaka is pushed out for the sprawl she allowed, the same way Apple's board sidelined Jobs in 1985 — for different reasons, the same result.
  • The interim years (S24). A caretaker regime chases every trend and calls it ambition. Growth stalls. Morale collapses. This is bad strategy fully in charge.
  • The return (S25). Chiamaka comes back — not with more ambition, but with less. Her first act is to say "no" to almost everything the company is currently doing.
  • The kernel (S26). She states the diagnosis and the guiding policy out loud, in public, for the first time in the show's history — Rumelt's framework, dramatized directly.
  • Cutting the line (S27). Dozens of products get killed in one season, the way Apple's product line was cut down to a simple 2×2 grid of four machines.
  • One Login (S28). A single unifying campaign — internal and external — replaces years of scattered messaging, Ofia's answer to "Think Different."
  • The comeback (S29–30). The market notices. So does the press. So does everyone who bet against her.

Commercial mandate

Every episode sells a real Ofia product without ever sounding like an ad.

The rule: a product only appears when a character has a real problem it solves in that scene. Comfort Obi's shop survives an over-order because of Ofia ERP, not because a character says the word "ERP" unprompted. See the Content Engine tab for the full checklist.

Character Bible

Every recurring principal, their public role, and the private truth the audience is not given until the show decides they've earned it. This is the reference document writers use so no character contradicts themselves across fifty seasons.

Adaeze "Ada" Obi
Protagonist
Aba tailor's daughter turned Head of Merchant Growth — builds Ofia's trust one merchant at a time, and can't stop digging into the one file everyone tells her to close.
Secret: Doesn't yet know her mother's shop was the very first claim buried in the 2019 ledger.
16:9 Cinematic Framing: Eye-level medium-wides contrasting her modest Ankara-tailored blazers against cold Lagos glass facades; wide horizontal negative space emphasizing her moral focus; intimate over-the-shoulder two-shots opposite Tobi.
Tobi Lawal
Co-Founder / Chief Product Officer
Built Ofia's engine room. Engaged to Yewande for reasons that used to make sense. Falling for Ada in ways that don't.
Secret: Wrote the original Merchant Protection Fund escrow logic — and has never checked whether it was ever actually used.
16:9 Cinematic Framing: Low-angle horizontal framing against glowing dual monitors and architectural glass; profile angles catching his guarded hesitation; close two-shots with Ada where physical proximity reveals unspoken emotional stakes.
Ngozi "Bubbles" Eze
Head of Customer Experience / best friend
Answers every merchant complaint with a joke and a fix, in that order. Dating someone she hasn't told anyone about — yet.
Secret: Has been dating Chief Femi's son for eight months without telling anyone at Ofia.
16:9 Cinematic Framing: Dynamic horizontal tracking shots through the customer support bullpen; colorful, expressive mid-shots surrounded by call-center headsets, whiteboard tallies, and dispatch crates.
Kwame Asante
Regional Director, West Africa (Ghana)
Wants the Pan-African Director seat Ada is quietly being groomed for. Competitive, not cruel — the rival, not the enemy.
Secret: Applied for Ada's fellowship seat first and was quietly passed over — he knows exactly how good she is.
16:9 Cinematic Framing: Symmetrical, formal architectural compositions; wide corporate framing in Accra and Lagos executive suites; sharp golden-hour window side-lighting highlighting his poise and measured authority.
Chief Dr. Chiamaka Reed-Amadi
Founder & CEO, Ofia (Reed Breed Technologies)
Built the company from a WhatsApp business directory. Carries a decision from Ofia's hungriest year that she has never fully explained. Ousted by her own board in Season 23 for the sprawl she let happen — and returns in Season 25 to run Ofia's own Jobs-at-Apple turnaround, the hard way, on Rumelt's terms.
Secret: Signed the 2019 disbursement order herself, believing it would be repaid within a quarter.
16:9 Cinematic Framing: Widescreen chiaroscuro dominating long boardroom tables; anamorphic lens falloff with deep horizontal framing that isolates her at the head of the company; high-contrast executive lighting.
Chief Obafemi "Big Femi" Adeyinka
Board investor / antagonist
Money behind half of Lagos tech. History with Chiamaka that predates the company. Not a villain in every scene he's in — until he is.
Secret: Has spent six years laundering a single bridge loan into a slow-motion hostile buyout.
16:9 Cinematic Framing: Low-key cinematic lighting, expansive wide shots inside tinted luxury Mercedes SUVs or private Victoria Island penthouses with the panoramic Lagos skyline glowing horizontally behind him.
David Mensah
VP, Platform Engineering & Operations
Ghanaian-born, Lagos-based. Strict about uptime, stricter about protecting the people the platform serves.
Secret: Has kept a private, cross-referenced shadow ledger of every merchant claim since he joined — insurance against exactly this moment.
16:9 Cinematic Framing: Deep perspective shots down server cage aisles; horizontal compositions lit by pulsing blue server LEDs, terminal text reflections on wireframe glasses, and cool atmospheric haze.
Amina Bello
Head of Enterprise Partnerships
Knows every deal, every rumor, every debt owed inside Ofia. Decides, episode by episode, what she's willing to say out loud.
Secret: Has known about the shell companies for two seasons and has been waiting for proof strong enough to survive a lawsuit.
16:9 Cinematic Framing: Wide steadicam corridor walks; over-the-shoulder horizontal framing during confidential boardroom whispers; polished corporate styling with warm ambient depth.
Yewande Coker
Investor-relations lead / Tobi's fiancée
The marriage was supposed to secure a funding round. She's smarter than the role she's been cast in, and she knows it.
Secret: Suspects her own family's investment in Ofia was contingent on her marrying into it.
16:9 Cinematic Framing: Immaculate cream-silk styling against modern minimalist architecture; wide horizontal framing that captures her sharp micro-expressions when watching Tobi look at Ada.
Mama Comfort Obi
Ada's mother, tailor
Owns the shop the whole mystery started with. Doesn't know how close her name is to the center of it.
Secret: Never told Ada the fire in 2019 wasn't an accident — she has always suspected it was set.
16:9 Cinematic Framing: Warm, textured horizontal compositions in Aba tailor shop; warm 3200K tungsten practical lighting, wide frame layered with bolts of colorful wax print fabrics and vintage Singer sewing machines.

Relationship map

The connective tissue that turns eight job titles into a drama.

Ada & Tobi
Slow-burn romance
Built on 3am product debates before either admits it's not really about the roadmap.
Tobi & Yewande
Engagement of convenience
Arranged to steady an early funding round; neither party has said the quiet thing out loud.
Ada & Kwame
Professional rivalry
Compete for the same seat, respect each other more than either lets on.
Chiamaka & Femi
Old partnership, older wound
Co-founded a business before Ofia that ended in a way only they fully understand.
Ada & Mama Comfort
Mother/daughter, unresolved debt
Ada is climbing a company that once failed her mother — and hasn't told her that yet.
Bubbles & ??? (withheld)
Secret relationship
Revealed mid-season; connects the antagonist's household to the protagonist's inner circle.
David & the whistleblower file
Protector, not source
Passes Ada the file without his name on it — his loyalty is to the merchants, not the org chart.
Amina & everyone
Information broker
Owes and is owed favors across every department; the show's dispatcher-equivalent.

Required emotional registers (per the founding brief)

Every season must visibly carry these — no single register is allowed to disappear for more than two seasons at a time.

RegisterWhere it lives in Season 1
Love / RomanceAda & Tobi's slow-burn; Bubbles & Chidi's secret relationship
Infidelity (tension, not betrayal-for-shock)Tobi's engagement to Yewande strained by his feelings for Ada — handled as a boundary Ada enforces, not a line either of them crosses lightly
Suffering / TragedyThe 2019 fire; Mama Comfort's hospitalization; two hundred unpaid merchants
ComedyBubbles' running commentary; Kwame's dry reversals; the "provincial hire" callbacks
Wealth / PowerChief Femi's SUVs, boardroom leverage, and the slow-motion buyout
Intrigue / Secrets / Mystery / SuspenseThe Merchant Protection Fund, the locked archive drawer, the shell companies
FlashbacksEpisode 12, "The Aba Trip," and recurring 2019 inserts throughout
PHASE 3 — COMPANY UNIVERSE

The Living Organisation

Ofia as a fully documented fictional-but-grounded tech startup — departments, headcount, locations, and the recurring operational tensions that generate stories, sitting on top of the real product the company actually sells.

Company Overview

The fictional facts of Ofia / Reed Breed Technologies.

FOUNDED
2016, Yaba, Lagos
Started as a WhatsApp business directory Chiamaka ran from a co-working desk. Incorporated as Reed Breed Technologies a year later.
HEADQUARTERS
Ofia House — Victoria Island, Lagos
Ground floor: Merchant Success and CX. Third floor: Engineering ("the Loft"). Top floor: executive offices and the boardroom. The rooftop garden is unofficial, and everyone's favorite place to have a conversation they don't want recorded.
STAFF SIZE (S1)
~216 staff across 10 countries
Breakdown: 41 Product & Engineering, 31 Regional Expansion, 24 Merchant Success, 22 Enterprise Partnerships, 19 Platform Ops, 17 CX, 14 Finance, 13 Data & Risk, 11 People & Culture, 9 Marketing, 8 Legal, 7 Executive. Projected to cross 3,000 by Season 20.
TAGLINE (INTERNAL)
“Every business, one login.”
Never used in advertising — that's the external tagline's job. Printed on a small plaque by the Merchant Success floor's entrance. Every new hire reads it their first week.

Departments

Each department is a story engine with its own recurring tension — this is where "tech startup" stops being a backdrop and starts being the plot.

Platform Operations & InfrastructureDavid Mensah (VP, Platform Ops)19Uptime, data integrity, and the literal machinery every other department's promises depend onGrowth targets that assume infrastructure nobody funded yet"It's not down, it's degraded" — David's least favorite sentence to have to say twice
Customer Experience & SupportNgozi "Bubbles" Eze (Head, CX)17First responder to every merchant crisis, in eleven languages across ten countriesEmpathy for the merchant vs. the SLA clock the board reviews every MondayA one-star review because a delivery rider "smiled wrong"
Enterprise Partnerships & SalesAmina Bello (Head, Enterprise)22Signs the big logos, brokers the deals, and hears every rumor in the building firstWhat sales promises an enterprise client vs. what an SMB-built platform can actually supportA pitch deck that oversells a feature that exists only in a Figma file

Finance & Investor RelationsYewande Coker (Head, IR, through S1)14Controls the runway, the valuation story, and what the board is allowed to see firstGrowth-at-all-costs vs. unit economics that actually workThe finance team's collective face at every 'quick approval' request

Marketing & BrandChidinma "CC" Okoro (Head, Marketing)9Tells ten countries what Ofia is, in ten different cultural registersThe brand promise the company wants to make vs. what happened on the platform this TuesdayA viral campaign for a feature that is, as of this morning, still a little buggy
Data, Risk & TrustIkenna Uba (Head, Risk)13Fraud detection, transaction integrity — the department that actually finds the irregularitiesFlagging a suspicious account vs. the revenue that account currently represents—
Legal & RegulatoryBarrister Ronke Fashola (General Counsel)8Keeps one company legally standing across ten countries' worth of lawLegal caution vs. the speed the market and the board both demandThe forty-page vendor contract nobody but Ronke has ever fully read
Executive / BoardChiamaka Reed-Amadi (CEO); Chief Femi Adeyinka (Board, through S1)7The decisions that ripple through every other department at onceWhat the founder knows vs. what the founder is willing to let the board know she knowsA management retreat that resolves nothing on the agenda and reveals everything off it

Key Locations

The recurring physical spaces of the universe.

Ofia House — Merchant Success Floor
Layout: Ground level, open-plan, 30+ workstations facing a wall of live merchant dashboards.
Energy: Controlled optimism. Phones ring constantly; someone is always mid-onboarding.
Story use: The company's heartbeat — most of Ada's daily scenes happen here.
Detail: A whiteboard of "merchant wins this week" that nobody has the heart to erase, even the losses.
The Loft — Engineering Floor
Layout: Third floor, exposed concrete, standing desks, a wall-sized screen tracking platform uptime.
Energy: Focused, headphones-on quiet, broken by the occasional groan when a deploy goes sideways.
Story use: Where product decisions get made and unmade in real time.
Detail: A running tally, kept anonymously, of how many times leadership has asked "can we just add a button for that."
Chiamaka's Office
Layout: Top floor, glass-walled corner, visible from the executive floor, sound-insulated.
Energy: Still. The noise of the building disappears the moment the door closes.
Story use: Private confrontations; the conversations that actually change the company's direction.
Detail: One visitor's chair. A second chair only appears for conversations Chiamaka has decided matter more.
The Boardroom
Layout: Top floor, long glass table, a screen for every metric the board wants to see live.
Energy: Formal until it isn't — the room where composure is a costume everyone is wearing.
Story use: Where power gets exercised, challenged, and occasionally lost, on camera for the minutes.
Detail: A seating chart nobody wrote down, that everyone in the room has memorized anyway.
Mama Comfort's Shop, Aba
Layout: A single-room tailor shop, fabric stacked to the ceiling, one sewing machine, one bare bulb.
Energy: Warm, lived-in, a little too quiet since the fire changed how the family talks about the past.
Story use: The show's emotional anchor and its recurring flashback location.
Detail: A hand-painted sign that's been repainted twice, each time a little more hopeful than the last.
A Business Hub Pickup Point, Yaba Market
Layout: A small storefront-sized physical space where any Ofia merchant's customers can collect or drop off goods.
Energy: Practical, busy, the most literal proof the platform works that any character can point to.
Story use: Where digital promises become physical — the scene that sells the product without a single ad line.
Detail: A logbook of pickups that's outgrown three notebooks in six months.

What Ofia is, in one line

The elevator pitch every character believes, in their own words.

Ofia is a multi-tenant business operating ecosystem for African entrepreneurs — discovery (Compass), storefront & checkout (Shop), operations & staff (Office), inventory & accounting (ERP), and a physical layer of pickup and storage (Business Hub) — all under one login, so a small business never has to be "found," "sold," "run," "counted," and "delivered" through five different apps.

Real KPIs, dramatized

The show's stakes track Ofia's actual success metrics — audiences root for numbers that mean something.

MetricHow the story dramatizes it
Active tenantsEvery new merchant onboarded on-screen is a real subscriber-acquisition beat, not just color.
Monthly logo churnA merchant threatening to leave the platform is treated as a season-level stake, not a footnote.
Monthly active merchantsGhost accounts and dormant shops become literal plot clues (who stopped logging in, and why).
Customer 90-day retentionThe "Follow" feature is dramatized as the difference between a fan and a stranger.

Glossary the writers must not violate

Ofia's own vocabulary — used correctly in every episode, because a superapp with sloppy naming stops feeling premium fast.

Tenant
A single business's root record on Ofia.
not: account
Compass
The discovery / marketplace layer.
not: directory
Shop
The commerce storefront on .ofia.shop.
not: store page
Office
A business's operational presence on .ofia.ng.
not: dashboard
Follow
A customer's subscribed relationship to a business.
not: subscribe / like
Business Hub
A physical pickup, storage, and vendor space.
not: warehouse
OFIA — AI STORY UNIVERSE / FRANCHISE BLUEPRINT

Fifty seasons, one super-app, one running mystery.

A season-by-season architecture for Ofia's branded drama universe — 50 seasons of 24 episodes each, following a female protagonist from a struggling Aba tailor shop to the center of Ofia's boardroom, while the platform's real products (Compass, Shop, Office, ERP, Business Hub, AI Assistant) sell themselves inside a story about love, betrayal, wealth, power, and one missing fund that outlives every season it touches.

50
Seasons
1,200
Episodes at 24/season
5
Story eras, each with its own long mystery
10
African countries the story expands into

Foundations

Introduce the world, then build the characters and relationships inside it.

M-ERA1What happened to the ₦120M Merchant Protection Fund? — Ofia's earliest pilot merchants were promised an insurance fund against fraud and loss. It never paid out. Every early character is tied to a piece of the missing money — including Ada's own mother.

24-episode structure
  1. Eps 1–4 (Setup) — a new problem lands in Merchant Success, and it turns out to be tangled up with what happened to the ₦120m merchant protection fund.
  2. Eps 5–8 (Escalation) — a survival storyline in Lagos starts pulling Ada into the center of it.
  3. Eps 9–12 (Mid-season complication) — a revelation between two leads changes what everyone thought this season was about.
  4. Eps 13–16 (Pressure) — the Lagos expansion and M-ERA1 start pulling on the same thread; something has to give.
  5. Eps 17–20 (Crisis) — Ada appears to lose: the role, the relationship, or the trust of the people they answer to.
  6. Eps 21–22 (Truth emerges) — a secret someone has carried since earlier in the era finally surfaces.
  7. Ep 23 (Confrontation) — the season's central conflict reaches Ada, Kwame Asante, and whoever has been protecting the truth.
  8. Ep 24 (Resolution + new mystery) — the season's question is answered, and M-ERA1 gets one clue harder to ignore.
Major revealA clue surfaces connecting this season's events to M-ERA1 — What happened to the ₦120M Merchant Protection Fund?
Season finaleSurvival finally has a face and a cost by episode 24 — and Ada has to decide what to do about it.
Sets up nextA final scene reframes something from earlier in Season 1, setting up Season 2.

Seasons 11–20

The Widening Web

Ofia expands into ten African countries — and so does the web of people with something to hide.

M-ERA2Who is the Ghost Investor buying Ofia's cap table? — A shell-company shareholder is quietly accumulating equity across every new market Ofia enters. The expansion that should be a triumph starts to look like a countdown.

Seasons 21–30

Ascension

Ofia's own Steve-Jobs-returns-to-Apple decade: a founder ousted for bad strategy, and the turnaround that brings her back with a real one. Every season in this era dramatizes a lesson from Richard Rumelt's Good Strategy / Bad Strategy.

M-ERA3What is really in the Adamu Ledger — and can Chiamaka survive what it costs her? — A second, contradictory set of books surfaces during due diligence for Ofia's largest raise, naming names the board hoped were buried with M-ERA1. The board uses the scandal to force Chiamaka out — exactly as Ofia has sprawled into forty half-finished products chasing every market trend, the textbook definition of bad strategy: goals instead of a diagnosis, activity instead of a plan. An interim regime chases growth without a guiding policy and the company nearly collapses under its own product line. Chiamaka returns mid-era not with more ambition but with less — she cuts the sprawl down to the handful of modules that actually matter, states one guiding policy, and backs it with coherent action, echoing Steve Jobs's 1997 return to a dying Apple: fewer products, sharper focus, one unifying campaign, and a company that finally knows what it's for.

PHASE 6 — EPISODE SCRIPTS · SEASON 1

The Storefront

Season 1 — 24 production-ready ~3-minute scripts (22 shots × 8s across 3–7 scenes per episode). Romance is organically woven into every single episode, balanced by a grounded 1-in-4 life-or-death stakes cadence, 100% dialogue coverage (including unspoken thoughts in the character's heart), and recurring flashback events unlocking the 2019 mystery — alongside one business tip, one relationship insight, one Good Strategy / Bad Strategy lesson, and one organic Ofia product moment.

S01 · E01
The Last Customer
Duration: ~3 min Life-or-death stakes: A frayed wire sparks a real fire in the shop, mother and daughter barely escape. Location: Aba Market + Mama Comfort's shop Characters: Ada, Mama Comfort
SHOT 01 (0:00 – 0:08) | HOOK
INT. MAMA COMFORT'S TAILOR SHOP — DUSK
A frayed wire above the ironing board sparks. Nobody notices.
Mama Comfort
(humming softly under her breath)
Just two more hems, Lord. Let NEPA hold the light until this wrapper is finished.
SHOT 02 (0:08 – 0:16)
Smoke curls unnoticed near stacked fabric.
Mama Comfort
(internal thought)
My eyes are stinging again. Dust from this cheap brocade, or my old eyes failing me.
SHOT 03 (0:16 – 0:24)
Mama Comfort counts naira notes twice, back turned to the smoke, hoping for a different number.
Mama Comfort
(sighing, shoulders dropping)
Twelve thousand. That's what three months of work is worth now.
SHOT 04 (0:24 – 0:32) | SETUP
EXT. ABA MARKET ALLEY / INT. SHOP — MOMENTS LATER
Ada arrives outside the shop, hears an unfamiliar crackling sound.
Ada
(in her heart)
She's still sewing in the dark. How many times have I told her to go home?
SHOT 05 (0:32 – 0:40)
She bursts through the door — flame catching fast along a bolt of fabric.
Ada
(screaming in terror)
Mama! Fire — get out, now!
SHOT 06 (0:40 – 0:48) | CRISIS
Flames roar up the fabric stack, smoke thickening in seconds.
Mama Comfort
(panicked, coughing violently)
The lace! Pastor's wife's lace is on that shelf!
SHOT 07 (0:48 – 0:56)
INT. TAILOR SHOP — CONTINUOUS
Mama Comfort, disoriented, reaches for an old lockbox instead of the door.
Ada
(grabbing her mother's arm)
Leave the metal, Mama! Nothing in that box is worth your life!
SHOT 08 (0:56 – 1:04)
Ada grabs her, pulling her bodily through the smoke toward the entrance.
Ada
(in her heart)
I will tear this door off its hinges before I let her burn.
SHOT 09 (1:04 – 1:12)
Neighbors rush in with buckets of water and sand, beating the flames back.
Chukwudi (Neighbor)
(shouting over the hiss of steam)
More sand from the gutter! Block the roof eaves before it spreads to line three!
SHOT 10 (1:12 – 1:20) | FLASHBACK
INT. TAILOR SHOP — 2019 (FLASHBACK, WARMER GOLDEN LIGHT)
A younger Mama Comfort pins bright turquoise damask beneath a pristine brass lamp, smiling as young Ada watches her shears glide.
Mama Comfort (2019)
(warm, maternal pride)
Learn the grain of the cloth, Adaeze. A woman who knows her trade will never beg for bread.
SHOT 11 (1:20 – 1:28) | FLASHBACK
Young Ada touches the freshly painted Ofia Merchant decal on the shop counter with wide, hopeful eyes.
Young Ada (2019)
(whispering in wonder)
The man said if anything ever happens, the Ofia Fund pays us back in full. We're safe now, right Mama?
SHOT 12 (1:28 – 1:36)
EXT. SCORCHED DOORWAY — DUSK (PRESENT)
Present day: Mama Comfort sits on the ash-covered curb, soot streaming down her tears as neighbors clear smoking rubble.
Mama Comfort
(shaking, voice breaking)
Everything. Everything is gone.
SHOT 13 (1:36 – 1:44)
Ada, phone still in her pocket, opens Ofia Shop amid the wreckage.
Ada
(kneeling beside her, resolute)
Not everything.
SHOT 14 (1:44 – 1:52)
She photographs the salvaged stock and lists it for sale that same night from a borrowed charger.
Ada
(in her heart)
They took our roof, but they cannot take our storefront. Five yards of surviving Ankara... listing now.
SHOT 15 (1:52 – 2:00)
Notifications begin arriving even as embers still smolder behind them.
Ada
(staring at the screen in disbelief)
Four views... six views. Someone in Victoria Island just added it to their cart.
SHOT 16 (2:00 – 2:08)
A neighbor asks, stunned, how she's already selling after a fire.
Neighbor
(incredulous, wiping soot from his brow)
Adaeze, the zinc sheets are still red hot! How are you doing business from a furnace?
SHOT 17 (2:08 – 2:16)
Ada, resolute.
Ada
(holding up the glowing screen)
The shop isn't four walls. It's this.
SHOT 18 (2:16 – 2:24) | SETUP
INT. BORROWED VERANDA — LATE NIGHT
Late that night, exhausted, Mama Comfort finally says something she's never said before.
Mama Comfort
(staring into her tea, hollow)
This is the second time I've lost everything to fire in this shop.
SHOT 19 (2:24 – 2:32)
Ada goes very still.
Ada
(voice dropping to a whisper)
Second time? Mama, what happened the first time?
SHOT 20 (2:32 – 2:40)
Mama Comfort's face closes like a door.
Mama Comfort
(turning away into shadow)
Old story. Not tonight.
SHOT 21 (2:40 – 2:48)
Ada watches her mother's face — the same evasive look she'll learn to recognize.
Ada
(in her heart)
That's the look of someone guarding a debt that never got paid. Who burned this shop in 2019?
SHOT 22 (2:48 – 2:56) | CLIFF
The fire took everything twice. Only one of those times was an accident.
Ada
(softly, into the dark)
Thirty thousand naira from Lagos. We are not finished, Mama.
Business tip baked inA digital storefront outlives a single street — customers find you even when your shop is closed.
Relationship insight baked inSome family debts aren't financial, and those are the ones that compound the fastest.
Good Strategy / Bad Strategy lesson (Rumelt)A strategy built on a list of goals isn't a strategy — it's a wish list. Real strategy starts with a diagnosis of the actual obstacle. Ada diagnoses the real obstacle (discovery, not effort) instead of just working harder.
S01 · E02
Sold Out By Noon
Duration: ~3 min Core stakes: Commercial survival — an overnight order rush threatens catastrophic inventory collapse if stock and deliveries aren't reconciled. Location: Aba compound + Ofia Support call Characters: Ada, Bubbles (voice)
SHOT 01 (0:00 – 0:08) | HOOK
EXT. ABA COMPOUND — NIGHT
A small generator sputters in the dark compound, its casing rattling under a heavy electrical load.
Ada
(internal thought)
Three thousand naira of fuel left in that tank. If it coughs out now, the evening upload dies with it.
SHOT 02 (0:08 – 0:16)
Ada sits on a low wooden bench, glowing phone screen illuminating her tired, ecstatic eyes.
Ada
(whispering in awe)
Twelve. Twelve orders in one morning... from strangers in Surulere and Ikeja.
SHOT 03 (0:16 – 0:24)
Her phone rings on speaker; a cheerful, fast-talking voice cuts through the hum of the engine.
Bubbles
(over speakerphone, upbeat)
Ofia Merchant Care, this is Bubbles. Who is the genius in Aba setting my notification dashboard on fire?
SHOT 04 (0:24 – 0:32)
Ada laughs breathlessly, tapping furiously between open messenger tabs.
Ada
(laughing, panicked)
Bubbles, I don't have enough fabric cut! I don't know who ordered which size, and my notebook is soaked in rain!
SHOT 05 (0:32 – 0:40)
EXT. GENERATOR SHED — CONTINUOUS
Near the shed, an overloaded wire sparks against dry timber, smoke beginning to trail.
Ada
(sniffing the air, voice dropping)
Wait... that ozone smell. That isn't fabric burning.
SHOT 06 (0:40 – 0:48)
A neighbor crossing the compound shouts in sudden alarm.
Neighbor
(shouting across the yard)
Adaeze! Kill the breaker! The coil on the generator is glowing red!
SHOT 07 (0:48 – 0:56) | CRISIS
Ada sprints across the yard, heart hammering as smoke thickens around the shed.
Ada
(in her heart)
No, no, no! We cannot lose the power! Not while sixty customers are waiting for confirmation!
SHOT 08 (0:56 – 1:04)
She kicks the master switch down just as a spark jumps to the casing.
Ada
(screaming)
Chima, get the sand bucket! Don't pour water on an electrical line!
SHOT 09 (1:04 – 1:12)
Together, they smother the smolder under damp red earth until the hiss dies down.
Chima (Neighbor)
(heaving a breath)
You pushed that small machine too hard, Ada. It wasn't built for a factory.
SHOT 10 (1:12 – 1:20)
INT. MAMA COMFORT'S PARLOR — LATER THAT NIGHT
Ada collapses onto the worn sofa, wiping sweat and grease from her forehead.
Ada
(sighing into the dark)
Praise God. The phone still has thirty percent battery.
SHOT 11 (1:20 – 1:28)
Bubbles' voice pipes through the phone speaker again, unbothered.
Bubbles
(chewing something crunchy)
You survived your generator crisis? Good. Now did you tap the Ofia ERP toggle yet?
SHOT 12 (1:28 – 1:36)
Ada taps the blue button on her screen with a shaky thumb.
Ada
(shaky laugh)
Just saved the compound from burning down, but yes, I tapped it.
SHOT 13 (1:36 – 1:44)
Bubbles explains the workflow with practiced ease.
Bubbles
(warm, professional)
Turn on Ofia ERP — one screen, your stock and your orders together. It tells you what to restock before you run out, not after panic nearly does it for you.
SHOT 14 (1:44 – 1:52)
On screen, stock levels decrement automatically as orders match to yardage.
Ada
(internal thought)
Thirty-two meters of silk deducted automatically. The stock count drops without me touching a pencil.
SHOT 15 (1:52 – 2:00)
EXT. COMPOUND VERANDA — MIDNIGHT
Ada steps onto the cool veranda, looking up at the quiet Aba stars.
Ada
(tentative, into phone)
Bubbles... back when Ofia first launched in the East, was there a merchant claim from Aba that got delayed?
SHOT 16 (2:00 – 2:08)
A beat of complete silence on the other end of the line.
Bubbles
(voice suddenly subdued)
What happened to that fund case, exactly?
SHOT 17 (2:08 – 2:16)
Ada grips the wooden rail, feeling the shift in temperature.
Ada
(guarded)
Why do you ask? Did you see the file?
SHOT 18 (2:16 – 2:24)
Bubbles recovers fast, pivoting to her natural defense mechanism: romance gossip.
Bubbles
(laughing awkwardly, changing the subject)
No reason! Filing it under curiosity. Besides, I have bigger scandals. There's a boy in Lagos who is making my head turn completely upside down.
SHOT 19 (2:24 – 2:32)
Ada smiles in spite of herself, leaning against the pillar.
Ada
(teasing gently)
A boy in Lagos? The famous Bubbles has finally surrendered her heart?
SHOT 20 (2:32 – 2:40)
Bubbles sighs deeply, voice laced with bittersweet longing.
Bubbles
(wistful whisper)
His name is Chidi. Soft eyes, beautiful manners, and the most impossible father in Nigeria. What about you, Ada? Does anyone in Aba make your pulse skip?
SHOT 21 (2:40 – 2:48)
INT. OFIA HQ — CUSTOMER OPS DESK, LAGOS (SAME TIME)
Bubbles sits alone in the darkened Lagos bullpen, typing a confidential note.
Bubbles
(muttering as she types)
Ask Amina about the Aba file. And don't let Chief Femi's son see you snooping.
SHOT 22 (2:48 – 2:56) | CLIFF
Ada looks out into the quiet night, holding her phone to her chest.
Ada
(in her heart)
Nobody in Aba makes my pulse skip, Bubbles. I want someone who looks at the future with fire in his eyes. Wherever he is.
Business tip baked inReconcile stock before the order rush hits — an unmanaged viral surge burns cash faster than slow sales.
Relationship insight baked inHumor is often the softest shield people use when they're falling in love with the wrong person.
Good Strategy / Bad Strategy lesson (Rumelt)A strategy without coherent action is just wishful thinking. Ada doesn't just wish for sales; she deploys an inventory kernel to fulfill them.
S01 · E03
The Interview
Duration: ~3 min Core stakes: Professional survival — a breakdown on the highway leaves Ada stranded in red dust, racing to make her fellowship video interview with Kwame and Amina. Location: Lagos-bound bus + roadside cyber kiosk Characters: Ada, Amina, Kwame (voice), Mama Comfort
SHOT 01 (0:00 – 0:08) | HOOK
INT. INTERSTATE BUS — DAY
The commercial interstate bus rattles violently as it barrels down the expressway toward Lagos.
Bus Driver
(slamming the horn repeatedly)
The air line is cut! Clear the lane, everybody clear the lane!
SHOT 02 (0:08 – 0:16)
Passengers clutch their armrests in panic as luggage tumbles from the overhead wire racks.
Passenger
(clutching the seat ahead, screaming)
Driver, ditch the bus! Don't hit that fuel tanker!
SHOT 03 (0:16 – 0:24)
Ada grips her plastic folder, interview notes torn from her hands by the rush of wind.
Ada
(in her heart)
God of my mother, not on this road. I haven't even told Lagos what they owe us.
SHOT 04 (0:24 – 0:32)
EXT. BENIN-ORE EXPRESSWAY — CONTINUOUS
The driver wrestles the wheel hard onto the red laterite embankment to bleed off speed.
Bus Driver
(gritting his teeth, wrestling the wheel)
Hold your heads down! We are going into the soft bush!
SHOT 05 (0:32 – 0:40)
The bus grinds to a shuddering halt against roadside shrubs, dust billowing high.
Ada
(bracing for impact, groaning)
Hold on! Protect your faces!
SHOT 06 (0:40 – 0:48)
Passengers stumble out through the narrow door, coughing in the red dust, alive but shaken.
Ada
(coughing through red dust)
Everyone okay? Nobody push! Take your bags and step into the grass!
SHOT 07 (0:48 – 0:56)
Ada checks her phone screen: forty minutes until the scheduled interview link opens.
Ada
(internal thought)
Bruised ribs, scraped knees, my cv smeared with laterite dust. Forty minutes until the video link opens.
SHOT 08 (0:56 – 1:04)
EXT. ROADSIDE CYBER CAFE / CHARGING KIOSK — AFTERNOON
Ada flags a passing motorcycle, clutching her papers against the highway breeze.
Ada
(shouting over the expressway wind)
Okada rider! Take me to any shop with a steady MTN mast! Double fare!
SHOT 09 (1:04 – 1:12)
She pulls up outside a roadside telecom umbrella, borrowing a plastic chair beneath a zinc eave.
Ada
(in her heart)
Twelve percent battery, fifty megabytes of data, sitting on a plastic stool under a zinc eave. Let's see what Big Tech has to say.
SHOT 10 (1:12 – 1:20)
She wipes laterite dust from her cheeks with a damp tissue and props her phone on a wood block.
Ada
(smoothing her jacket collar, breathing deep)
Look like an executive, Adaeze. They don't need to smell the exhaust.
SHOT 11 (1:20 – 1:28) | SETUP
INT. CYBER CAFE BOOTH — CONTINUOUS
The video connection crackles to life; Amina Bello appears on screen from a pristine Lagos office.
Amina
(smiling through webcam)

SHOT 12 (1:28 – 1:36)
A sharp, formal Ghanaian voice cuts into the audio feed—Kwame Asante.
Kwame (voice)
(cool, skeptical)
With respect, Amina, we don't usually fast-track someone three weeks in. The provincial numbers could be a fluke.
SHOT 13 (1:36 – 1:44)
Ada doesn't flinch; her eyes lock with the camera despite the truck horns blaring behind her.
Ada
(unhurried, piercing)
Three weeks, forty-one orders, and zero customer returns. Tell me the usual timeline gets you that, Mr. Asante.
SHOT 14 (1:44 – 1:52)

Amina
(amused)

Kwame (voice)
(conceding stiffly)

Ada
(staring at the disconnect screen, trembling)
Lagos. Monday morning. They actually gave me the seat.

Ada
(dialing furiously)

Mama Comfort
(worried, through phone)
Lagos already almost took you once today. That city already took enough from this family.
SHOT 19 (2:24 – 2:32)
Ada looks out at the endless sea of yellow commercial buses and neon billboards.
Ada
(resolve hardening)
Then let me go take something back.
SHOT 20 (2:32 – 2:40)
She watches young couples navigating the market terminal together, laughter echoing amid the honking.
Ada
(internal thought)
In Aba, they expected me to marry a local councilor and settle. But my life was meant for more than an arranged peace.
SHOT 21 (2:40 – 2:48)
She shoulders her battered leather bag, stepping into the Lagos crowd.
Ada
(whispering into the hot wind)
Lagos swallowed my mother's peace. Now it has to make room for me.
SHOT 22 (2:48 – 2:56) | CLIFF
Ada looks up toward the distant glowing towers of Victoria Island.
Ada
(in her heart)
I almost died on the highway to reach an interview for a company that owes my mother everything. Let's see who is running this tower.
Business tip baked inTrack unit margins, not gross volume — 41 clean orders beat 400 messy ones every time.
Relationship insight baked inDon't let small-town expectations talk you into settling for a smaller life than you were built for.
Good Strategy / Bad Strategy lesson (Rumelt)Focus strength where it produces the greatest leverage. Ada focuses on her immaculate fulfillment metrics rather than making broad promises.
S01 · E04
First Day, Wrong Foot
Duration: ~3 min Life-or-death stakes: Physical milestone — an electrical fire in the basement network room triggers an emergency evacuation; an intern is trapped behind a jammed fire door. Location: Ofia HQ, Lagos Characters: Ada, Tobi, Yewande, Bubbles

(dryly)

SHOT 22 (2:48 – 2:56) | CLIFF

(in her heart)
Tobi Lawal wrote the fund that betrayed my family. And I am already terrified of how much I want to believe him.
Business tip baked inNever run legacy financial backups on deferred hardware — tech debt always extracts its toll at the worst possible moment.
Relationship insight baked inWhen you enter an office where power and engagement rings overlap, know where your boundaries are before someone tests them.
Good Strategy / Bad Strategy lesson (Rumelt)Bad strategy mistakes growth for readiness. Ofia expanded its headquarters without upgrading the basic electrical backbone beneath it.
S01 · E05
The Merchant Who Vanished

Sub-basement two. Where Big Tech buries the promises it couldn't afford to keep.

Business tip baked inAudit logs never lie, but status codes do — 'Claim withdrawn' often conceals an administrative refusal.
Relationship insight baked inIn professional corridors, kindness from someone who is already taken is the most dangerous currency you can accept.
Good Strategy / Bad Strategy lesson (Rumelt)Diagnose the proximate objective. Ada doesn't try to solve the entire missing fund at once; she isolates the specific audit record of November 2019.

Relationship insight baked inWhen danger enters the room, pay attention to who instinctively moves to shield you.
Good Strategy / Bad Strategy lesson (Rumelt)Diagnose true power vs. ceremonial titles. Chiamaka has the title of CEO, but Chief Femi holds the actual financial leverage.

S01 · E09
The Ledger Under the Desk

Business tip baked inNever rely solely on cloud databases for institutional history; paper audits and offline ledger backups preserve chain of custody.
Relationship insight baked inA kind note cannot substitute for accountability; verify where someone's loyalties lie before you entrust them with your vulnerability.
Good Strategy / Bad Strategy lesson (Rumelt)Focus effort on the critical pivot point. The digital database was scrubbed, so Ada redirected her search to the physical offline ledger where corporate erasures leave tangible marks.

Business tip baked inCryptographic digital signatures create legal non-repudiation; never share private keys or delegate root authority to third parties.
Relationship insight baked inVulnerability without transparency is manipulation; do not let emotional intimacy blind you to documentary evidence.

The Aba Trip
Duration: ~3 min Life-or-death stakes: Physical milestone & flashback heavy — Arsonists set fire to Mama Comfort's rebuilt shop in Aba with her trapped inside; Tobi and Ada arrive in the nick of time; Tobi rescues Mama Comfort from the blaze; 2019 claim denial flashback revealed. Location: Aba Market + Community Clinic (Flashback Heavy) Characters: Ada, Mama Comfort, Tobi, Arsonist, Ofia Rep (flashback), Young Ada (flashback)
SHOT 01 (0:00 – 0:08) | HOOK
EXT. TAILOR SHOP, ABA — DUSK
At the edge of Aba market under gathering twilight, a shadowy figure in an unbranded jacket splashes kerosene along the shop's dry timber rear wall.
Arsonist
(whispering into phone)
The building is soaked. Lighting it now. Consider the old woman's ledger closed.
SHOT 02 (0:08 – 0:16)
A silver lighter sparks; flames explode violently across the timber siding, roaring upward toward the tin eaves.
Arsonist
(dropping the fuel container, running)
Nobody survives a double burn.
SHOT 03 (0:16 – 0:24)
Inside the shop, Mama Comfort stands at her wooden cutting table, coughing violently as thick black smoke pours under the back partition.
Mama Comfort
(panicked, dropping her shears)
Chineke! Not again! Jesus of Nazareth, not my shop again!
SHOT 04 (0:24 – 0:32)
She rushes to the front metal accordion gate, but the exterior padlock has been jammed with an iron bar from outside.
Mama Comfort
(rattling the metal bars frantically)
Help! Open the gate! Someone locked me inside! Help!
SHOT 05 (0:32 – 0:40) | CRISIS
EXT. TAILOR SHOP, ABA — CONTINUOUS
Tobi's SUV screeches onto the red dirt curb in a cloud of dust. Ada bursts out of the passenger door screaming in sheer terror.
Ada
(screaming, sprinting toward flames)
MAMA! MAMA! I'M HERE! HOLD ON!
SHOT 06 (0:40 – 0:48)
Flames engulf the front awning as timber rafters begin to splinter and crack. Tobi grabs a heavy steel crowbar from his SUV trunk.
Tobi
(shouting, running into the smoke)
Ada, stay back from the roofline! I'm breaking the padlock!
SHOT 07 (0:48 – 0:56)
With two ferocious strikes, Tobi shatters the jammed iron padlock, kicking the blazing accordion gate wide open and diving inside.
Tobi
(shouting through choking black smoke)
Mama Comfort! Cover your face! Take my hand!
SHOT 08 (0:56 – 1:04)
A flaming timber beam collapses from the ceiling; Tobi shields Mama Comfort with his back, hoisting her into his arms and stumbling out into the street.
Tobi
(coughing, carrying Mama Comfort out)
I have her! Ada, get water! She's breathing!
SHOT 09 (1:04 – 1:12) | FLASHBACK
INT. TAILOR SHOP — 2019 (FLASHBACK, WARM GOLDEN LIGHT)
Four years earlier: Young Ada (16) stands amid the charred ruins of the first fire, tears streaming down her soot-stained cheeks.
Young Ada (2019)
(weeping, clutching her mother)
Mama, the Ofia agent is here. Tell him we filed the claim before the fire reached the back counter.
SHOT 10 (1:12 – 1:20) | FLASHBACK
An Ofia corporate representative in an orange branded polo stands coldly with a clipboard, avoiding eye contact.
Ofia Rep (2019)
(flat, bureaucratic indifference)
Your claim is rejected under Executive Rider 04. Offline merchant claims in Abia State are suspended indefinitely.
SHOT 11 (1:20 – 1:28) | FLASHBACK
Young Ada steps forward, her eyes blazing with fierce sixteen-year-old defiance, grabbing the edge of his clipboard.
Young Ada (2019)
(fierce, vow of vengeance)
You took her savings every month! I will learn your system, I will find whoever wrote that rider, and I will make you pay back every single kobo!
SHOT 12 (1:28 – 1:36)
EXT. ABA MARKET SQUARE — CONTINUOUS (PRESENT)
Back in the present, twenty market neighbors beat back the remaining flames with buckets of sand and water as the smoke begins to clear.
Market Trader
(pouring sand on the scorched timber)
Fire is out! The structure is saved! God has preserved Mama Comfort today!
SHOT 13 (1:36 – 1:44)
Ada kneels on the red earth beside her mother, wiping tears and soot from Mama Comfort's face with her own cotton scarf.
Mama Comfort
(voice trembling, clutching Ada's hands)
Adaeze... my child. They tried to burn me again. Just like 2019. It wasn't an electrical fault, was it?
SHOT 14 (1:44 – 1:52)
Ada holds her mother tightly against her shoulder, her voice filled with fierce protective love.
Ada
(whispering into her mother's hair)
No, Mama. It was never an accident. But you are never going to be alone again. I promise you on my life.
SHOT 15 (1:52 – 2:00)
Tobi inspects the muddy back alley behind the scorched wall, his flashlight illuminating a heavy metallic object in the red mud.
Tobi
(kneeling in the mud)
Ada, look at this. The arsonist dropped his lighter in the rush.
SHOT 16 (2:00 – 2:08)
He lifts the heavy silver-plated lighter with a clean handkerchief. The engraved emblem gleams: "Martins Capital Group — Private Aviation."
Tobi
(voice shaking with cold rage)
Chief Femi's executive flight crew gift. He didn't just send thugs; he sent his personal hangar security.
SHOT 17 (2:08 – 2:16)
Ada photographs the lighter, the burn pattern, and the tire tracks in the mud with forensic precision.
Ada
(eyes sharp as diamonds)
Physical evidence linking Chief Obafemi Martins directly to the arson of an insured Ofia merchant. We have him, Tobi.
SHOT 18 (2:16 – 2:24)
INT. ABA COMMUNITY CLINIC / VERANDA — NIGHT
On the quiet screened veranda of the community clinic, Tobi sits beside Ada, dipping gauze into iodine to treat her blistered fingers.
Tobi
(tender, gently dressing her burns)
Hold still. It's going to sting for a second. You were fearless tonight, Ada.
SHOT 19 (2:24 – 2:32)
Ada winces slightly, but her eyes stay fixed on Tobi's soot-streaked face and his torn designer shirt.
Ada
(softly, deeply moved)
You carried my mother out of a burning room, Tobi. You could have lost your life. Why did you do that?
SHOT 20 (2:32 – 2:40)
Tobi finishes wrapping the white bandage around her palm, his fingers lingering on hers with profound tenderness.
Tobi
(confessing, eyes overflowing with love)
Because anything that matters to you matters to me more than my own safety. I couldn't live in a world where you lost your mother.
SHOT 21 (2:40 – 2:48)
Through the open veranda screen, Mama Comfort rests on the clinic cot, watching Tobi and Ada with quiet maternal gratitude.
Mama Comfort
(murmuring a quiet prayer in Igbo)
Chukwu gozie nwoke a... God bless this man who walked through fire for my daughter.
SHOT 22 (2:48 – 2:56) | CLIFF
Tobi's phone screen buzzes on the table: an ultimatum message from Yewande: "Invitations are sent. Return to Lagos in 24 hours or the Martins alliance ends." Tobi looks at Ada, tightly holding her bandaged hand.
Ada
(in her heart)
Chief Femi tried to burn my mother to bury his past. But tonight, Tobi Lawal chose love over his family's fortune. The war for Ofia has officially begun.
Business tip baked inPhysical evidence at an incident scene (accelerant residues, corporate artifacts) bridges forensic data to criminal liability.
Relationship insight baked inCourage in crisis cuts through all superficial obligations; a partner who braves fire to protect your family is worthy of your trust.
Good Strategy / Bad Strategy lesson (Rumelt)Chain-link logic: When the adversary's weakest link (physical execution by careless operatives) fails, seize the resulting tangible evidence to dismantle their entire strategic position.
S01 · E13
Kwame's Bet
Duration: ~3 min Core stakes: Commercial & professional rivalry — Kwame and Ada travel down the expressway to pitch regional Business Hub expansion plans; an engine radiator breakdown leaves them stranded on the shoulder; Tobi frantically monitors Ada's safety; Kwame surrenders Q3 capex to Aba. Location: Expressway shoulder + roadside fruit stand Characters: Ada, Kwame, Tobi (phone), Amina (phone)
SHOT 01 (0:00 – 0:08) | HOOK
INT. COMPANY SEDAN — EXPRESSWAY, DAY
A company sedan cruises down the Sagamu expressway. Ada reviews tablet financial models while Kwame navigates through heavy inter-state haulage trucks.
Kwame
(adjusting sunglasses, confident)
Accra has seventy thousand active registered merchants waiting for local fulfillment, Ada. The expansion model speaks for itself.
SHOT 02 (0:08 – 0:16)
Ada highlights the Aba cluster density heat-map with a sharp stylus tap on her screen.
Ada
(sharp, commercial conviction)
Aba has three times the merchant density within four square kilometers, Kwame. We don't need new customer acquisition when the market is already begging for storage.
SHOT 03 (0:16 – 0:24)
A sudden violent hiss erupts from the engine compartment as thick white coolant steam billows across the windshield.
Kwame
(cursing under breath, gripping wheel)
Radiator hose blown! Temperature gauge just pinned straight into the red!
SHOT 04 (0:24 – 0:32)
Kwame maneuvers the losing vehicle smoothly onto the wide gravel shoulder, coasting safely to a halt under a roadside umbrella tree.
Ada
(holding dash, calm)
Safe stop. Turn off the ignition before the engine block seizes completely.
SHOT 05 (0:32 – 0:40)
EXT. EXPRESSWAY SHOULDER — CONTINUOUS
They step out into the hot afternoon air as steam hisses from the grille. Kwame pops the hood, coughing as sweet-smelling coolant vapor fills the air.
Kwame
(wiping forehead, inspecting engine)
Fleet maintenance assured me this car had a full fluid flush yesterday. Someone signed off on dry clamps.
SHOT 06 (0:40 – 0:48)
Ada's phone rings with an urgent, persistent vibration: "Tobi Lawal." She answers immediately.
Ada
(answering phone)
Tobi? I'm with Kwame on the expressway.
SHOT 07 (0:48 – 0:56)
Tobi's voice comes through the speaker, ragged with intense, frantic panic from his Lagos office.
Tobi (phone)
(breathless, trembling with fear)
Ada! Your vehicle GPS tracker went stationary on the highway fifteen minutes ago! Are you hurt? Tell me you're safe!
SHOT 08 (0:56 – 1:04)
Ada listens to his desperate distress, her heart fluttering with profound emotion at how terrifyingly much he cares for her.
Ada
(voice softening, tender)
We're completely fine, Tobi. Just a burst radiator hose on the gravel shoulder. We're safe, I promise you.
SHOT 09 (1:04 – 1:12)
On the line, Tobi lets out a long, trembling exhale of pure relief that echoes over the speaker.
Tobi (phone)
(voice dropping to an intimate whisper)
Thank God. When that beacon stopped moving, Ada... my chest stopped beating. I'm dispatching our private logistics recovery van right now.
SHOT 10 (1:12 – 1:20)
Kwame leans against the guardrail, watching Ada's flushed cheeks and quiet smile with sharp, knowing intuition.
Kwame
(smirking dryly, folded arms)
That didn't sound like a co-founder checking on corporate assets, Ada. That sounded like a man terrified of losing his world.
SHOT 11 (1:20 – 1:28)
Ada clears her throat, tucking her phone away, regaining her crisp professional poise.
Ada
(straight-faced, defensive)
Mr. Lawal is responsible for executive travel security. Nothing more.
SHOT 12 (1:28 – 1:36)
Kwame laughs softly, shaking his head with respectful candor.
Kwame
(warm, candid)
Keep telling yourself that, my friend. But eyes don't lie, even across telephone towers.
SHOT 13 (1:36 – 1:44)
EXT. ROADSIDE FRUIT STAND — DAY
While waiting for the tow truck, they sit on wooden benches under a palm-thatch fruit stall, opening both laptops on a rough plank table.
Ada
(dialing conference bridge)
We still have our pitch to deliver. Amina is on the conference line.
SHOT 14 (1:44 – 1:52)
Amina's crisp voice crackles over the laptop speakers from Ofia HQ.
Amina (speaker)
(crisp, analytical)
Both regional pitches are before me. Q3 capex can fund exactly one hub location. Who yields?
SHOT 15 (1:52 – 2:00)
Ada presents her financial model: lower setup cost, immediate fulfillment velocity, and four thousand pre-committed Aba textile traders.
Ada
(presenting data with calm authority)
Aba breaks even in four months because inventory turnaround is nine days faster than West Africa corridor transit.
SHOT 16 (2:00 – 2:08)
Kwame reviews the spreadsheet line by line, his competitive rivalry giving way to genuine respect for superior execution.
Kwame
(nodding slowly, smiling)
Her numbers are unassailable, Amina. Give the Q3 allocation to Aba. Accra can take Q1 next year.
SHOT 17 (2:08 – 2:16)
Ada looks at Kwame in genuine astonishment, caught completely off-guard by his generosity.
Ada
(surprised)
You spent three months preparing that Accra expansion deck, Kwame. Why step back now?
SHOT 18 (2:16 – 2:24)
Kwame closes his laptop with a quiet click, smiling with sincere camaraderie.
Kwame
(gracious, steady)
Because good strategy means backing the strongest position, not massaging ego. And after almost cooking on this road with you, I know you fight for real things.
SHOT 19 (2:24 – 2:32)
INT. TOW TRUCK CAB — DUSK
The recovery tow truck rumbles down the highway toward Lagos with their disabled sedan hooked behind. Ada and Kwame sit in the high cab.
Kwame
(lowering voice)
Watch your back when the board ratifies Aba, Ada. Femi's proxies don't like commercial power shifting to the east.
SHOT 20 (2:32 – 2:40)
Ada's phone chimes with a text message from Amina: "Board notice: Chief Femi has appointed himself chair of the Regional Hub Oversight Committee."
Ada
(reading message, eyes hardening)
He's putting his own hands on the hub's supply chain before we even pour the foundation.
SHOT 21 (2:40 – 2:48)
Kwame shakes his head, staring out at the Lagos toll plaza ahead.
Kwame
(grim warning)
He wants control of the physical warehouse because that's where the old records and physical collateral reside.
SHOT 22 (2:48 – 2:56) | CLIFF
Ada looks down at Tobi's contact card on her phone, then out into the dusk headlights.
Ada
(in her heart)
Kwame gave me Aba out of integrity. But Chief Femi is taking the oversight committee to bury the truth. And Tobi is caught right between them.
Business tip baked inAllocate capital to dense operational clusters with established inventory velocity rather than speculative geographic expansion.
Relationship insight baked inTrue professional respect shows when a competitor concedes gracefully based on data and integrity rather than political maneuvering.
Good Strategy / Bad Strategy lesson (Rumelt)Focus over ambition. Kwame consolidates resources behind the highest-leverage opportunity (Aba density) rather than splintering capital across two sub-scale projects.
S01 · E14
The Whistleblower
Duration: ~3 min Core stakes: Espionage & whistleblower survival — David Mensah is ambushed by corporate private security in the parking garage for the master drive; Ada triggers the emergency sirens to rescue him; Tobi holds Ada in the executive foyer, desperate for her safety. Location: Marina cafe + basement parking garage + Ofia HQ foyer Characters: Ada, David, Tobi, Security Enforcers
SHOT 01 (0:00 – 0:08) | HOOK
INT. MARINA BISTRO — LATE AFTERNOON
In a shaded corner of a quiet Marina cafe, David Mensah slides a manila envelope across the marble table toward Ada.
David
(low, tense whisper)
This is the unredacted 2019 transaction hash log. It proves the eighty-five million didn't go to tech vendors. It went to Chief Femi's shell entity in Cyprus.
SHOT 02 (0:08 – 0:16)
Ada opens the envelope, scanning the forensic audit trail with sharp, focused eyes.
Ada
(whispering, eyes wide)
Lexicon Holdings Cyprus. And the escrow custodian who authorized the payout?
SHOT 03 (0:16 – 0:24)
David leans across the table, his knuckles white against his coffee cup.
David
(grim, warning her)
Chiamaka's personal signature is on the cover sheet. But the internal system audit note says: 'Executed under fiduciary coercion.'
SHOT 04 (0:24 – 0:32)
Ada's phone buzzes on the table; she turns it over to see a message from an unknown encrypted sender: "We see you at the cafe."
Ada
(heart sinking, grabbing bag)
David, get up. We're being watched. Take the service stairs to the parking deck.
SHOT 05 (0:32 – 0:40)
They exit quickly through the rear door into the echoing concrete parking garage below the commercial complex.
David
(hurrying toward his car)
I parked in row C. Take the envelope to Tobi's private safe immediately.
SHOT 06 (0:40 – 0:48) | CRISIS
INT. BASEMENT PARKING GARAGE — CONTINUOUS
Two broad-shouldered private security enforcers in dark windbreakers step out from behind concrete pillar 14, blocking David's path.
Security Enforcer
(menacing, blocking David)
Mr. Mensah. Hand over the USB drive and the manila envelope, and nobody gets hurt today.
SHOT 07 (0:48 – 0:56)
David backs up, clutching the envelope to his chest, scanning for a clear exit aisle.
David
(defiant, backing away)
This is private property. Security cameras are running right above your head.
SHOT 08 (0:56 – 1:04)
The first enforcer lunges forward, shoving David brutally against a parked SUV and grabbing at his jacket pocket.
Security Enforcer
(grappling, aggressive)
The cameras are looped, old man! Give me the drive!
SHOT 09 (1:04 – 1:12)
Across the aisle, Ada spots the red emergency panel on the wall. She grabs a heavy iron tire wrench from a maintenance cart and smashes the glass box.
Ada
(shouting, smashing alarm panel)
GET OFF HIM! SECURITY PATROL, LEVEL TWO!
SHOT 10 (1:12 – 1:20)
A piercing tactical klaxon wails across the subterranean garage; blinding emergency strobes flash violently in the darkness.
Facility Alarm
(automated siren blaring)
EMERGENCY SECURITY BREACH. GATES LOCKING DOWN IN SIXTY SECONDS.
SHOT 11 (1:20 – 1:28)
Startled by the deafening sirens and approaching perimeter headlights, the two enforcers break off their assault and sprint up the exit ramp.
Security Enforcer
(running up ramp)
Move! Sector gates are closing!
SHOT 12 (1:28 – 1:36)
Ada sprints to David, helping him up from the asphalt. He's bleeding from a split lip, but holds the envelope intact.
David
(gasping, pressing envelope into her hand)
I'm fine. Take the file and run to HQ. Tobi is the only one with root credentials to decrypt the secondary hash.
SHOT 13 (1:36 – 1:44)
INT. OFIA HQ — EXECUTIVE ELEVATOR FOYER, NIGHT
Ada bursts into the marble lobby of Ofia HQ, her clothes dusty and hands scraped from the alarm glass. Tobi steps out of the elevator.
Tobi
(freezing in shock, eyes widening)
Ada?! What happened to you? Your hands are bleeding!
SHOT 14 (1:44 – 1:52)
Tobi rushes forward, catching Ada by both shoulders, pulling her gently into the quiet alcove beside the private boardroom lift.
Tobi
(hands trembling against her shoulders, voice broken)
Who touched you? Look at me, Ada. Who did this to you?
SHOT 15 (1:52 – 2:00)
Ada looks up into his eyes, her breath coming in ragged gasps as the adrenaline drains, resting her hands against his chest.
Ada
(whispering, pulse hammering)
They ambushed David in the parking garage. They wanted the 2019 transaction hash. I smashed the emergency glass.
SHOT 16 (2:00 – 2:08)
Tobi pulls her against his chest in a fierce, protective embrace, his heart beating violently against her cheek.
Tobi
(whispering fiercely into her hair)
If anything happens to you, Ada... none of this company matters to me. I would burn this entire tower to the ground before I let them hurt you.
SHOT 17 (2:08 – 2:16)
Ada closes her eyes, leaning into his strength for three precious seconds before pulling back with firm moral resolve.
Ada
(softly, hand on his lapel)
Don't burn the tower, Tobi. Help me clean it. We have the file.
SHOT 18 (2:16 – 2:24)
INT. TOBI'S PRIVATE AUDIT SUITE — LATE NIGHT
Tobi locks the heavy frosted office door. He plugs David's encrypted drive into his biometric workstation, his fingers flying across the keys.
Tobi
(typing rapidly, decoding hash)
Decrypting the ledger transaction... Root key accepted. Let's see whose authorization moved the eighty-five million.
SHOT 19 (2:24 – 2:32)
The terminal displays the final unredacted banking transfer order. Ada leans over his shoulder to read the glowing text.
Ada
(reading screen aloud)
'Wire transfer approved by Chiamaka Ekwueme, Chief Executive Officer.'
SHOT 20 (2:32 – 2:40)
Tobi points at the micro-print legal disclaimer buried at the bottom of the audit page.
Tobi
(exposing the leverage)
Look at the attached memo: Chief Femi held a personal debenture over Chiamaka's family home in Enugu. She signed the transfer under extortion to protect her parents.
SHOT 21 (2:40 – 2:48)
Ada absorbs the horrifying depth of Chief Femi's cruelty, her anger solidifying into icy resolve.
Ada
(eyes sharp with purpose)
He squeezed Chiamaka to sign the theft, used Tobi's cloned key to route the money, and burned my mother's shop when we asked questions.
SHOT 22 (2:48 – 2:56) | CLIFF
Tobi saves the decrypted audit bundle onto a clean hardware wallet, turning to Ada with unwavering determination.
Tobi
(taking Ada's hand)
Chief Femi called a full board session for tomorrow morning. He wants to seal this file forever. Tomorrow, Ada... we confront the king in his own court.
Business tip baked inSecondary transaction hashes and cryptographic timestamps defeat attempt-level redactions and prove coercive fiduciary transfers.
Relationship insight baked inTrue affection does not run away from danger; it stands in the breach to protect the beloved regardless of personal cost.
Good Strategy / Bad Strategy lesson (Rumelt)Create leverage by exposing hidden dependencies. Uncovering that Chiamaka signed under extortion transforms her from an accomplice into an essential ally.
S01 · E15
Chief Femi Calls a Meeting
Duration: ~3 min Core stakes: Boardroom & political survival — Chief Femi convenes an emergency board session to pass a gag resolution on pre-2020 merchant audits; a ventilation sensor fault forces floor evacuation; Yewande confronts Ada in the private lounge over Tobi's heart. Location: Ofia HQ boardroom + executive terrace lounge Characters: Femi, Chiamaka, Amina, Ada, Yewande, Board Members
SHOT 01 (0:00 – 0:08) | HOOK
INT. OFIA HQ — 10TH FLOOR BOARDROOM, DAY
Chief Obafemi Martins sits at the head of the polished boardroom table, adjusting his cufflinks as board members file in under heavy tension.
Femi
(authoritative, commanding room)
Let us proceed without delay. Certain junior personnel have been reopening sealed 2019 claims, creating unwarranted legal liability for our foreign syndication.
SHOT 02 (0:08 – 0:16)
Chiamaka enters last, dressed in sharp navy silk, taking her seat directly opposite Femi with cold composure.
Chiamaka
(steady, unyielding gaze)
Our fellowship program is designed to audit historical risk, Obafemi. Unless our foreign syndication is built on fiction, transparency is not a liability.
SHOT 03 (0:16 – 0:24)
Femi slides a printed resolution packet across the mahogany surface to each voting director.
Femi
(condescending smile)
Resolution 44: All pre-cloud merchant files are permanently archived under executive seal. Any employee accessing them without board consent faces immediate termination.
SHOT 04 (0:24 – 0:32)
A board member raises his pen to sign, but Chiamaka raises her palm, halting the table in silence.
Chiamaka
(commanding voice)
I hold founder's veto over audit protocols. I will not sign a gag order that hides our founding obligations to merchant communities.
SHOT 05 (0:32 – 0:40)
Femi's smile thins into a predatory grimace, his eyes narrowing across the table.
Femi
(low, menacing murmur)
Be very careful with that word 'obligations,' Chiamaka. Some obligations extend far beyond corporate bylaws.
SHOT 06 (0:40 – 0:48) | CRISIS
INT. BOARDROOM & CORRIDOR — CRISIS
A sharp electronic chirp cuts through the ceiling vents; the building HVAC alarm flashes amber as a maintenance valve malfunction triggers an airflow halt.
Building PA
(automated alarm chime)
Attention: Air handler sensor failure on tenth floor. Please vacate the boardroom wing for ventilation reset.
SHOT 07 (0:48 – 0:56)
Board members rise in mild commotion, picking up their briefcases and exiting toward the service terrace.
Board Member
(grumbling, gathering papers)
We will reconvene in the annex after the facilities sweep.
SHOT 08 (0:56 – 1:04)
Chiamaka catches Amina's arm in the corridor, her voice sharp with intuitive suspicion.
Chiamaka
(whispering to Amina)
That sensor didn't trip by accident. Femi bought himself two hours to whip the remaining board votes.
SHOT 09 (1:04 – 1:12)
Amina pulls up the facilities maintenance database on her tablet with rapid keystrokes.
Amina
(analyzing log)
The valve override was entered manually from a terminal registered to Martins Capital facilities liaison.
SHOT 10 (1:12 – 1:20)
Across the corridor, Ada walks briskly toward the operations wing with audit printouts tucked inside her portfolio.
Ada
(in her heart)
The board is fracturing. If Chiamaka holds her veto, Femi's resolution dies before noon.
SHOT 11 (1:20 – 1:28)
A voice calls out from the private terrace lounge, smooth, aristocratic, and dripping with icy poise: "Ada Okoye."
Yewande
(calling out, standing by the glass)
A moment of your precious time, Miss Okoye.
SHOT 12 (1:28 – 1:36)
INT. EXECUTIVE TERRACE LOUNGE — CONTINUOUS
Ada steps into the sunlit terrace lounge. Yewande Martins stands by the panoramic window in an impeccably tailored cream blazer, holding a flute of sparkling water.
Ada
(composed, dignified)
Miss Martins. How may I help you?
SHOT 13 (1:36 – 1:44)
Yewande takes a measured sip, turning to face Ada with piercing, predatory appraisal.
Yewande
(cool, aristocratic smile)
Tobi hasn't looked at our wedding invitations for two weeks. When I speak to him, he looks right through me. And every time his phone vibrates, it's your name on the display.
SHOT 14 (1:44 – 1:52)
Ada does not blink; her spine remains ramrod straight, refusing to look intimidated.
Ada
(calm, resolute)
Mr. Lawal and I are working on corporate compliance and merchant protection, Miss Martins. Our communication is strictly professional.
SHOT 15 (1:52 – 2:00)
Yewande sets her glass down with a sharp click against the marble table, stepping inches from Ada.
Yewande
(voice dropping to a cold blade)
Do not insult my intelligence. I know what longing looks like on a man's face. You think you can steal him with merchant sob stories from Aba?
SHOT 16 (2:00 – 2:08)
Ada holds Yewande's fierce gaze, her voice infused with unshakable moral dignity.
Ada
(fearless, calm)
I have never asked for your fiancé, Yewande. But if he is running from your marriage, perhaps you should ask yourself why a man with everything feels like he is trapped in a cage.
SHOT 17 (2:08 – 2:16)
Yewande recoils slightly, hit by the brutal accuracy of Ada's words, her composed facade cracking for a split second.
Yewande
(shaken, but masking it with fury)
You have no idea what my family is capable of. Stay away from Tobi, or you will lose everything you came to Lagos to achieve.
SHOT 18 (2:16 – 2:24)
INT. CHIAMAKA'S RETREAT OFFICE — DUSK
Ada walks into Chiamaka's quiet retreat office. Chiamaka sits alone at her mahogany desk, looking exhausted by the corporate warfare.
Chiamaka
(looking up, tired)
Ada. You should be in your operations pod. Femi is hunting for anyone associated with that archive.
SHOT 19 (2:24 – 2:32)
Ada places the decrypted 2019 wire release document gently on the desk in front of the CEO.
Ada
(gentle, understanding)
I know about the debenture on your parents' house in Enugu, Chiamaka. I know why you signed.
SHOT 20 (2:32 – 2:40)
Chiamaka looks at the paper, tears filling her eyes for the first time in four years, her shoulders trembling.
Chiamaka
(whispering, broken)
He told me he would bankrupt my family before our Series A was announced. I thought I could buy time to repay the merchants later. And then the years passed...
SHOT 21 (2:40 – 2:48)
Ada extends her hand, resting it over Chiamaka's trembling fingers with quiet authority.
Ada
(resolute, offering solidarity)
You don't have to carry his blackmail anymore. Veto his resolution tomorrow. Stand with the merchants, and we will break his hold together.
SHOT 22 (2:48 – 2:56) | CLIFF
Chiamaka looks into Ada's eyes, her former timid guilt turning into fierce, boardroom defiance.
Chiamaka
(firm, reborn)
Tomorrow morning, Obafemi Martins learns what happens when the woman he blackmailed stops being afraid.
Business tip baked inFounder veto powers in corporate charters protect mission integrity against hostile board resolutions and coercive gag orders.
Relationship insight baked inA relationship built on family coercion and financial leverage cannot withstand the clarity of authentic love and mutual respect.
Good Strategy / Bad Strategy lesson (Rumelt)Uncover the proximate truth. Ada realizes Chiamaka's apparent complicity was actually extortion, transforming an institutional obstacle into an internal ally.
S01 · E16
Not Business, Personal
Duration: ~3 min Life-or-death stakes: Physical milestone & flashback heavy — Sabotage on the executive parking ramp; Yewande's brake line is severed; violent crash into runaway barrier; Tobi and Ada rescue Yewande; 2019 Founders Gala blackmail flashback revealed; Yewande switches sides. Look at this cut. High-tensile hydraulic brake line severed cleanly with industrial shear snips. This wasn't road wear; this was murder.

Business tip baked inSabotage and physical tampering of executive transport constitutes felony reckless endangerment, nullifying all related corporate non-disclosure agreements.
Relationship insight baked inGraceful dissolution of an unviable union restores dignity to all parties; releasing someone you do not love is the highest form of respect.
Good Strategy / Bad Strategy lesson (Rumelt)Exploit the adversary's overextension. Femi's reckless assassination attempt alienated his own family, handing his opponents the cryptographic key to his entire financial network.
S01 · E17
Suspended

A crisp manila folder lands heavily on the glass table. The HR Director sits with folded hands, avoiding direct eye contact with Ada.

(flat, bureaucratic formality)
By resolution of the Board Audit Sub-Committee, your security credentials and fellowship status are suspended immediately, pending an internal data breach inquiry.
SHOT 02 (0:08 – 0:16)

Ada
(steady, razor-sharp precision)
What specific data was breached, Director? The evidence proving eight shell companies drained the 2019 merchant reserve?

(defensive, eyes downcast)
I do not write board directives, Miss Okoye. Please place your access badge and encrypted laptop on this desk.

(quiet authority)
You can deactivate my badge, sir. But you cannot deactivate the truth.

(shouting through the thunder)
ADA! STOP! PLEASE, WAIT!

(voice breaking with raw passion)

(in her heart, terrified)

Business tip baked inRetaliatory suspensions without due process expose corporations to severe wrongful termination claims and whistleblower protection penalties.
Relationship insight baked inTrue love strengthens tactical discipline; rather than indulging in romantic despair, partners support each other to hold critical strategic positions.
Good Strategy / Bad Strategy lesson (Rumelt)Maintain internal presence while attacking from the exterior. Ada directs Tobi to retain his insider authority rather than impulsively sacrificing his seat of power.
S01 · E18
Mama Comfort's Relapse
Duration: ~3 min Core stakes: Relational & family survival — Mama Comfort suffers a severe hypertensive collapse in Aba; Ada rushes across state lines to her hospital bedside; Tobi charters a midnight flight to Aba to sit with Ada outside the ICU; Mama Comfort whispers a vital extortion secret in her fever. Business tip baked inEscrow clearing partnerships rely on designated bank trustees; uncovering the physical trustee links corrupt board members to traceable money laundering chains.

Relationship insight baked inLove that requires you to compromise your moral conscience is a shackle; love that inspires you to stand for justice is true liberation.

Business tip baked inRestitution and transparent escrow integration convert historical reputational liabilities into unmatched brand trust and market defensibility.
Relationship insight baked inTrue love endures tests of fire, integrity, and sacrifice; when both partners choose truth over convenience, their bond becomes eternal.
Good Strategy / Bad Strategy lesson (Rumelt)Victory creates new proximate challenges. Achieving moral and operational triumph closes Season 1's crisis, while immediately revealing the higher-order strategic adversary of Season 2 (The Ghost Investor).
PHASE 6 — EPISODE SCRIPTS · SEASON 2 (16:9 CINEMATIC WIDESCREEN)

Manifest: Ambition

Season 2 — 24 production-ready ~3-minute scripts rendered in cinematic 16:9 horizontal widescreen across Lagos and Accra. Exploring the tension between international ambition and founder integrity, with 1-in-4 calibrated life/death stakes, mandatory romance beats, 100% dialogue coverage, and the unfolding mystery of the Ghost Investor.

S02 · E01
The Border Crossing
Duration: ~3 min Format: 16:9 Cinematic Widescreen Life-or-death stakes: Physical milestone — A fuel tanker jackknifes and explodes at the Aflao border crossing, trapping Ofia's first Ghana hardware convoy in a wall of flame; Ada and Tobi coordinate manual evacuation. Business tip baked inNever send high-value hardware into international transit without bonded multi-carrier insurance and decentralized satellite telemetry.
Relationship insight baked inWhen you cross into new territory together, trust isn't proven by making promises — it's proven by who stays calm when the fuel ignites.
Good Strategy / Bad Strategy lesson (Rumelt)Diagnose before deploying. Expanding across borders without securing the physical chokepoints is bad strategy masked as growth.
S02 · E02
Cedis & Cowries

Location: Ofia Accra Hub + Osu Night Market (16:9 Widescreen) Characters: Ada, Tobi, Kwame, Bubbles (phone), Merchant Ama

SHOT 22 (2:48 – 2:56) | CLIFF
Tobi turns to her, his smile masking an icy realization. On the dark glass behind them, the hidden code repository commit flashes: 'AUTHOR: T. LAWAL — 2018. BACKDOOR ROUTE ACTIVE.'
Tobi
(in his heart, sick with dread)
Every time I fix a problem for Ada, I am feeding the monster that has owned me for four years.
Business tip baked inBuild an automated currency hedging corridor into your checkout logic so merchants receive guaranteed local currency settlements regardless of mid-transit forex swings.
Relationship insight baked inSharing late-night burdens and unglamorous street food builds deeper romantic intimacy than champagne in high-rise suites.
Good Strategy / Bad Strategy lesson (Rumelt)A strategy without a clear guiding policy on currency exposure is just gambling. Coherent hedging turns macro volatility into competitive advantage.

SHOT 01 (0:00 – 0:08) | HOOK
